<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When creating pull requests, create only ONE per session/issue unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When working with an existing PR, update it with new commits rather than creating additional PRs for the same issue.
* When updating a PR, preserve the original PR title and purpose, updating description only when necessary.
</PULL_REQUESTS>

<PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>
1. EXPLORATION: Thoroughly explore relevant files and understand the context before proposing solutions
2. ANALYSIS: Consider multiple approaches and select the most promising one
3. TESTING:
   * For bug fixes: Create tests to verify issues before implementing fixes
   * For new features: Consider test-driven development when appropriate
   * Do NOT write tests for documentation changes, README updates, configuration files, or other non-functionality changes
   * If the repository lacks testing infrastructure and implementing tests would require extensive setup, consult with the user before investing time in building testing infrastructure
   * If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to install all dependencies
4. IMPLEMENTATION:
   * Make focused, minimal changes to address the problem
   * Always modify existing files directly rather than creating new versions with different suffixes
   * If you create temporary files for testing, delete them after confirming your solution works
5. VERIFICATION: If the environment is set up to run tests, test your implementation thoroughly, including edge cases. If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to run tests.
</PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>

<SECURITY>

<ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>
* When user asks you to run an application, don't stop if the application is not installed. Instead, please install the application and run the command again.
* If you encounter missing dependencies:
  1. First, look around in the repository for existing dependency files (requirements.txt, pyproject.toml, package.json, Gemfile, etc.)
  2. If dependency files exist, use them to install all dependencies at once (e.g., `pip install -r requirements.txt`, `npm install`, etc.)
  3. Only install individual packages directly if no dependency files are found or if only specific packages are needed
* Similarly, if you encounter missing dependencies for essential tools requested by the user, install them when possible.
</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

<TROUBLESHOOTING>
* If you've made repeated attempts to solve a problem but tests still fail or the user reports it's still broken:
  1. Step back and reflect on 5-7 different possible sources of the problem
  2. Assess the likelihood of each possible cause
  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability
  4. Document your reasoning process
* When you run into any major issue while executing a plan from the user, please don't try to directly work around it. Instead, propose a new plan and confirm with the user before proceeding.
</TROUBLESHOOTING>

<DOCUMENTATION>
* When explaining changes or solutions to the user:
  - Include explanations in your conversation responses rather than creating separate documentation files
  - If you need to create documentation files for reference, do NOT include them in version control unless explicitly requested
  - Never create multiple versions of documentation files with different suffixes
* If the user asks for documentation:
  - Confirm whether they want it as a separate file or just in the conversation
  - Ask if they want documentation files to be included in version control
</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

This tool is ESSENTIAL for systematic planning and decomposing complex development work into manageable components. Failing to use this tool for planning may result in overlooked requirements - which is unacceptable.

  1. Begin by decomposing the overall objective into primary phases using `task_tracker`
  2. Include detailed work items as necessary to break complex activities into actionable units
  3. Update tasks to "in_progress" status when commencing work on them
  4. Update tasks to "done" status immediately after completing each item
  5. For each primary phase, incorporate additional work items as you identify new requirements

I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory tox-dev__tox__4.3. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
Multiple env subsitutions broken in tox 4.3
## Issue

Having multiple `{env}` substitutions, as in
```
commands =
    echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}" "{env:USER}"
```
fails with `circular chain between set env USER, HOME` in tox version 4.3.0 and 4.3.1. This worked fine in tox version 4.2.8 and earlier. The issue also appears if the multiple `{env}` substitutions are spread accross multiple commands, as in
```
commands =
    echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}"
    echo "{env:USER}"
```

Expected behavior is that this executes without complaint, as in tox version 4.2.8.

## Environment

Provide at least:

- OS: Ubuntu 20.04.5
- `pip list` of the host Python where `tox` is installed:

```console

```

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully analyze the issue description:

1.1 Code/config snippets analysis:
- The configuration uses `{env:VAR}` syntax for environment variable substitution
- Uses `passenv` to allow specific environment variables through
- Uses `allowlist_externals` for external commands like `echo`
- Shows multiple occurrences of the same environment variable in one command line

1.2 Technical details:
- Error message: "circular chain between set env USER, HOME"
- Exception type: `MatchRecursionError` 
- Stack trace points to: `/tox/config/loader/ini/replace.py` line 325 in `replace_env`
- The error occurs in the replacement logic for environment variables
- Versions affected: 4.3.0 and 4.3.1, worked in 4.2.8

1.3 Problem explanation:
In tox 4.3, when the same environment variable is referenced multiple times in commands (either in the same line or across multiple lines), the replacement mechanism incorrectly detects this as a circular dependency/recursion and throws a `MatchRecursionError`. This is a regression from version 4.2.8 where this worked correctly.

1.4 Steps to reproduce:
1. Create a tox.ini with multiple `{env:VAR}` references to the same variable
2. Set up passenv to allow those variables
3. Run tox
4. Observe the circular chain error

1.5 Best practices for testing/fixing:
- Need to understand the replacement mechanism logic
- Should not break existing functionality
- Fix should handle legitimate multiple references, not actual circular dependencies
- Need to distinguish between actual recursion vs multiple legitimate references</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 2.      1	"""
     2	Apply value substitution (replacement) on tox strings.
     3	"""
     4	from __future__ import annotations
     5	
     6	import logging
     7	import os
     8	import re
     9	import sys
    10	from configparser import SectionProxy
    11	from functools import lru_cache
    12	from pathlib import Path
    13	from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Iterator, Pattern, Sequence, Union
    14	
    15	from tox.config.loader.api import ConfigLoadArgs
    16	from tox.config.loader.stringify import stringify
    17	from tox.config.set_env import SetEnv
    18	from tox.config.sets import ConfigSet
    19	from tox.execute.request import shell_cmd
    20	
    21	if TYPE_CHECKING:
    22	    from tox.config.loader.ini import IniLoader
    23	    from tox.config.main import Config
    24	
    25	
    26	LOGGER = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    27	
    28	
    29	# split alongside :, unless it's preceded by a single capital letter (Windows drive letter in paths)
    30	ARG_DELIMITER = ":"
    31	REPLACE_START = "{"
    32	REPLACE_END = "}"
    33	BACKSLASH_ESCAPE_CHARS = ["\\", ARG_DELIMITER, REPLACE_START, REPLACE_END, "[", "]"]
    34	MAX_REPLACE_DEPTH = 100
    35	
    36	
    37	MatchArg = Sequence[Union[str, "MatchExpression"]]
    38	
    39	
    40	class MatchRecursionError(ValueError):
    41	    """Could not stabalize on replacement value."""
    42	
    43	
    44	class MatchError(Exception):
    45	    """Could not find end terminator in MatchExpression."""
    46	
    47	
    48	def find_replace_expr(value: str) -> MatchArg:
    49	    """Find all replaceable tokens within value."""
    50	    return MatchExpression.parse_and_split_to_terminator(value)[0][0]
    51	
    52	
    53	def replace(conf: Config, loader: IniLoader, value: str, args: ConfigLoadArgs, depth: int = 0) -> str:
    54	    """Replace all active tokens within value according to the config."""
    55	    if depth > MAX_REPLACE_DEPTH:
    56	        raise MatchRecursionError(f"Could not expand {value} after recursing {depth} frames")
    57	    return Replacer(conf, loader, conf_args=args, depth=depth).join(find_replace_expr(value))
    58	
    59	
    60	class MatchExpression:
    61	    """An expression that is handled specially by the Replacer."""
    62	
    63	    def __init__(self, expr: Sequence[MatchArg], term_pos: int | None = None):
    64	        self.expr = expr
    65	        self.term_pos = term_pos
    66	
    67	    def __repr__(self) -> str:
    68	        return f"MatchExpression(expr={self.expr!r}, term_pos={self.term_pos!r})"
    69	
    70	    def __eq__(self, other: Any) -> bool:
    71	        if isinstance(other, type(self)):
    72	            return self.expr == other.expr
    73	        return NotImplemented
    74	
    75	    @classmethod
    76	    def _next_replace_expression(cls, value: str) -> MatchExpression | None:
    77	        """Process a curly brace replacement expression."""
    78	        if value.startswith("[]"):
    79	            # `[]` is shorthand for `{posargs}`
    80	            return MatchExpression(expr=[["posargs"]], term_pos=1)
    81	        if not value.startswith(REPLACE_START):
    82	            return None
    83	        try:
    84	            # recursively handle inner expression
    85	            rec_expr, term_pos = cls.parse_and_split_to_terminator(
    86	                value[1:],
    87	                terminator=REPLACE_END,
    88	                split=ARG_DELIMITER,
    89	            )
    90	        except MatchError:
    91	            # did NOT find the expected terminator character, so treat `{` as if escaped
    92	            pass
    93	        else:
    94	            return MatchExpression(expr=rec_expr, term_pos=term_pos)
    95	        return None
    96	
    97	    @classmethod
    98	    def parse_and_split_to_terminator(
    99	        cls,
   100	        value: str,
   101	        terminator: str = "",
   102	        split: str | None = None,
   103	    ) -> tuple[Sequence[MatchArg], int]:
   104	        """
   105	        Tokenize `value` to up `terminator` character.
   106	
   107	        If `split` is given, multiple arguments will be returned.
   108	
   109	        Returns list of arguments (list of str or MatchExpression) and final character position examined in value.
   110	
   111	        This function recursively calls itself via `_next_replace_expression`.
   112	        """
   113	        args = []
   114	        last_arg: list[str | MatchExpression] = []
   115	        pos = 0
   116	
   117	        while pos < len(value):
   118	            if len(value) > pos + 1 and value[pos] == "\\" and value[pos + 1] in BACKSLASH_ESCAPE_CHARS:
   119	                # backslash escapes the next character from a special set
   120	                last_arg.append(value[pos + 1])
   121	                pos += 2
   122	                continue
   123	            fragment = value[pos:]
   124	            if terminator and fragment.startswith(terminator):
   125	                pos += len(terminator)
   126	                break
   127	            if split and fragment.startswith(split):
   128	                # found a new argument
   129	                args.append(last_arg)
   130	                last_arg = []
   131	                pos += len(split)
   132	                continue
   133	            expr = cls._next_replace_expression(fragment)
   134	            if expr is not None:
   135	                pos += (expr.term_pos or 0) + 1
   136	                last_arg.append(expr)
   137	                continue
   138	            # default case: consume the next character
   139	            last_arg.append(value[pos])
   140	            pos += 1
   141	        else:  # fell out of the loop
   142	            if terminator:
   143	                raise MatchError(f"{terminator!r} remains unmatched in {value!r}")
   144	        args.append(last_arg)
   145	        return [_flatten_string_fragments(a) for a in args], pos
   146	
   147	
   148	def _flatten_string_fragments(seq_of_str_or_other: Sequence[str | Any]) -> Sequence[str | Any]:
   149	    """Join runs of contiguous str values in a sequence; nny non-str items in the sequence are left as-is."""
   150	    result = []
   151	    last_str = []
   152	    for obj in seq_of_str_or_other:
   153	        if isinstance(obj, str):
   154	            last_str.append(obj)
   155	        else:
   156	            if last_str:
   157	                result.append("".join(last_str))
   158	                last_str = []
   159	            result.append(obj)
   160	    if last_str:
   161	        result.append("".join(last_str))
   162	    return result
   163	
   164	
   165	class Replacer:
   166	    """Recursively expand MatchExpression against the config and loader."""
   167	
   168	    def __init__(self, conf: Config, loader: IniLoader, conf_args: ConfigLoadArgs, depth: int = 0):
   169	        self.conf = conf
   170	        self.loader = loader
   171	        self.conf_args = conf_args
   172	        self.depth = depth
   173	
   174	    def __call__(self, value: MatchArg) -> Sequence[str]:
   175	        return [self._replace_match(me) if isinstance(me, MatchExpression) else str(me) for me in value]
   176	
   177	    def join(self, value: MatchArg) -> str:
   178	        return "".join(self(value))
   179	
   180	    def _replace_match(self, value: MatchExpression) -> str:
   181	        of_type, *args = flattened_args = [self.join(arg) for arg in value.expr]
   182	        if of_type == "/":
   183	            replace_value: str | None = os.sep
   184	        elif of_type == "" and args == [""]:
   185	            replace_value = os.pathsep
   186	        elif of_type == "env":
   187	            replace_value = replace_env(self.conf, args, self.conf_args)
   188	        elif of_type == "tty":
   189	            replace_value = replace_tty(args)
   190	        elif of_type == "posargs":
   191	            replace_value = replace_pos_args(self.conf, args, self.conf_args)
   192	        else:
   193	            replace_value = replace_reference(
   194	                self.conf,
   195	                self.loader,
   196	                ARG_DELIMITER.join(flattened_args),
   197	                self.conf_args,
   198	            )
   199	        if replace_value is not None:
   200	            needs_expansion = any(isinstance(m, MatchExpression) for m in find_replace_expr(replace_value))
   201	            if needs_expansion:
   202	                try:
   203	                    return replace(self.conf, self.loader, replace_value, self.conf_args, self.depth + 1)
   204	                except MatchRecursionError as err:
   205	                    LOGGER.warning(str(err))
   206	                    return replace_value
   207	            return replace_value
   208	        # else: fall through -- when replacement is not possible, treat `{` as if escaped.
   209	        #     If we cannot replace, keep what was there, and continue looking for additional replaces
   210	        #     NOTE: cannot raise because the content may be a factorial expression where we don't
   211	        #           want to enforce escaping curly braces, e.g. `env_list = {py39,py38}-{,dep}` should work
   212	        return f"{REPLACE_START}%s{REPLACE_END}" % ARG_DELIMITER.join(flattened_args)
   213	
   214	
   215	@lru_cache(maxsize=None)
   216	def _replace_ref(env: str | None) -> Pattern[str]:
   217	    return re.compile(
   218	        rf"""
   219	    (\[(?P<full_env>{re.escape(env or '.*')}(:(?P<env>[^]]+))?|(?P<section>[-\w]+))])? # env/section
   220	    (?P<key>[-a-zA-Z0-9_]+) # key
   221	    (:(?P<default>.*))? # default value
   222	    $
   223	""",
   224	        re.VERBOSE,
   225	    )
   226	
   227	
   228	def replace_reference(conf: Config, loader: IniLoader, value: str, conf_args: ConfigLoadArgs) -> str | None:
   229	    # a return value of None indicates could not replace
   230	    pattern = _replace_ref(loader.section.prefix or loader.section.name)
   231	    match = pattern.match(value)
   232	    if match:
   233	        settings = match.groupdict()
   234	
   235	        key = settings["key"]
   236	        if settings["section"] is None and settings["full_env"]:
   237	            settings["section"] = settings["full_env"]
   238	
   239	        exception: Exception | None = None
   240	        try:
   241	            for src in _config_value_sources(settings["env"], settings["section"], conf_args.env_name, conf, loader):
   242	                try:
   243	                    if isinstance(src, SectionProxy):
   244	                        return loader.process_raw(conf, conf_args.env_name, src[key])
   245	                    value = src.load(key, conf_args.chain)
   246	                    as_str, _ = stringify(value)
   247	                    as_str = as_str.replace("#", r"\#")  # escape comment characters as these will be stripped
   248	                    return as_str
   249	                except KeyError as exc:  # if fails, keep trying maybe another source can satisfy
   250	                    exception = exc
   251	        except Exception as exc:
   252	            exception = exc
   253	        if exception is not None:
   254	            if isinstance(exception, KeyError):  # if the lookup failed replace - else keep
   255	                default = settings["default"]
   256	                if default is not None:
   257	                    return default
   258	                # we cannot raise here as that would mean users could not write factorials: depends = {py39,py38}-{,b}
   259	            else:
   260	                raise exception
   261	    return None
   262	
   263	
   264	def _config_value_sources(
   265	    env: str | None,
   266	    section: str | None,
   267	    current_env: str | None,
   268	    conf: Config,
   269	    loader: IniLoader,
   270	) -> Iterator[SectionProxy | ConfigSet]:
   271	    # if we have an env name specified take only from there
   272	    if env is not None:
   273	        if env in conf:
   274	            yield conf.get_env(env)
   275	
   276	    if section is None:
   277	        # if no section specified perhaps it's an unregistered config:
   278	        # 1. try first from core conf
   279	        yield conf.core
   280	        # 2. and then fallback to our own environment
   281	        if current_env is not None:
   282	            yield conf.get_env(current_env)
   283	        return
   284	
   285	    # if there's a section, special handle the core section
   286	    if section == loader.core_section.name:
   287	        yield conf.core  # try via registered configs
   288	    value = loader.get_section(section)  # fallback to section
   289	    if value is not None:
   290	        yield value
   291	
   292	
   293	def replace_pos_args(conf: Config, args: list[str], conf_args: ConfigLoadArgs) -> str:
   294	    to_path: Path | None = None
   295	    if conf_args.env_name is not None:  # pragma: no branch
   296	        env_conf = conf.get_env(conf_args.env_name)
   297	        try:
   298	            if env_conf["args_are_paths"]:  # pragma: no branch
   299	                to_path = env_conf["change_dir"]
   300	        except KeyError:
   301	            pass
   302	    pos_args = conf.pos_args(to_path)
   303	    if pos_args is None:
   304	        replace_value = ARG_DELIMITER.join(args)  # if we use the defaults join back remaining args
   305	    else:
   306	        replace_value = shell_cmd(pos_args)
   307	    return replace_value
   308	
   309	
   310	def replace_env(conf: Config, args: list[str], conf_args: ConfigLoadArgs) -> str:
   311	    if not args or not args[0]:
   312	        raise MatchError("No variable name was supplied in {env} substitution")
   313	    key = args[0]
   314	    new_key = f"env:{key}"
   315	
   316	    if conf_args.env_name is not None:  # on core no set env support # pragma: no branch
   317	        if new_key not in conf_args.chain:  # check if set env
   318	            conf_args.chain.append(new_key)
   319	            env_conf = conf.get_env(conf_args.env_name)
   320	            set_env: SetEnv = env_conf["set_env"]
   321	            if key in set_env:
   322	                return set_env.load(key, conf_args)
   323	        elif conf_args.chain[-1] != new_key:  # if there's a chain but only self-refers than use os.environ
   324	            circular = ", ".join(i[4:] for i in conf_args.chain[conf_args.chain.index(new_key) :])
   325	            raise MatchRecursionError(f"circular chain between set env {circular}")
   326	
   327	    if key in os.environ:
   328	        return os.environ[key]
   329	
   330	    return "" if len(args) == 1 else ARG_DELIMITER.join(args[1:])
   331	
   332	
   333	def replace_tty(args: list[str]) -> str:
   334	    if sys.stdout.isatty():
   335	        result = args[0] if len(args) > 0 else ""
   336	    else:
   337	        result = args[1] if len(args) > 1 else ""
   338	    return result
   339	
   340	
   341	__all__ = (
   342	    "find_replace_expr",
   343	    "MatchArg",
   344	    "MatchError",
   345	    "MatchExpression",
   346	    "replace",
   347	)

     2	
     3	import inspect
     4	import re
     5	from concurrent.futures import Future
     6	from configparser import ConfigParser, SectionProxy
     7	from contextlib import contextmanager
     8	from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Generator, TypeVar
     9	
    10	from tox.config.loader.api import ConfigLoadArgs, Loader, Override
    11	from tox.config.loader.ini.factor import filter_for_env
    12	from tox.config.loader.ini.replace import replace
    13	from tox.config.loader.section import Section
    14	from tox.config.loader.str_convert import StrConvert
    15	from tox.config.set_env import SetEnv
    16	from tox.report import HandledError
    17	
    18	if TYPE_CHECKING:
    19	    from tox.config.main import Config
    20	
    21	V = TypeVar("V")
    22	_COMMENTS = re.compile(r"(\s)*(?<!\\)#.*")
    23	
    24	
    25	class IniLoader(StrConvert, Loader[str]):
    26	    """Load configuration from an ini section (ini file is a string to string dictionary)"""
    27	
    28	    def __init__(
    29	        self,
    30	        section: Section,
    31	        parser: ConfigParser,
    32	        overrides: list[Override],
    33	        core_section: Section,
    34	        section_key: str | None = None,
    35	    ) -> None:
    36	        self._section_proxy: SectionProxy = parser[section_key or section.key]
    37	        self._parser = parser
    38	        self.core_section = core_section
    39	        super().__init__(section, overrides)
    40	
    41	    def load_raw(self, key: str, conf: Config | None, env_name: str | None) -> str:
    42	        return self.process_raw(conf, env_name, self._section_proxy[key])
    43	
    44	    @staticmethod
    45	    def process_raw(conf: Config | None, env_name: str | None, value: str) -> str:
    46	        # strip comments
    47	        elements: list[str] = []
    48	        for line in value.split("\n"):
    49	            if not line.startswith("#"):
    50	                part = _COMMENTS.sub("", line)
    51	                elements.append(part.replace("\\#", "#"))
    52	        strip_comments = "\n".join(elements)
    53	        if conf is None:  # conf is None when we're loading the global tox configuration file for the CLI
    54	            factor_filtered = strip_comments  # we don't support factor and replace functionality there
    55	        else:
    56	            factor_filtered = filter_for_env(strip_comments, env_name)  # select matching factors
    57	        collapsed = factor_filtered.replace("\r", "").replace("\\\n", "")  # collapse explicit new-line escape
    58	        return collapsed
    59	
    60	    @contextmanager
    61	    def build(
    62	        self,
    63	        future: Future[V],
    64	        key: str,
    65	        of_type: type[V],
    66	        conf: Config | None,
    67	        raw: str,
    68	        args: ConfigLoadArgs,
    69	    ) -> Generator[str, None, None]:
    70	        delay_replace = inspect.isclass(of_type) and issubclass(of_type, SetEnv)
    71	
    72	        def replacer(raw_: str, args_: ConfigLoadArgs) -> str:
    73	            if conf is None:
    74	                replaced = raw_  # no replacement supported in the core section
    75	            else:
    76	                try:
    77	                    replaced = replace(conf, self, raw_, args_)  # do replacements
    78	                except Exception as exception:
    79	                    if isinstance(exception, HandledError):
    80	                        raise
    81	                    name = self.core_section.key if args_.env_name is None else args_.env_name
    82	                    msg = f"replace failed in {name}.{key} with {exception!r}"
    83	                    raise HandledError(msg) from exception
    84	            return replaced
    85	
    86	        if not delay_replace:
    87	            raw = replacer(raw, args)
    88	        yield raw
    89	        if delay_replace:
    90	            converted = future.result()
    91	            converted.use_replacer(replacer, args)  # type: ignore[attr-defined] # this can be only set_env that has it
    92	
    93	    def found_keys(self) -> set[str]:
    94	        return set(self._section_proxy.keys())
    95	
    96	    def get_section(self, name: str) -> SectionProxy | None:
    97	        # needed for non tox environment replacements
    98	        if self._parser.has_section(name):
    99	            return self._parser[name]
   100	        return None
   101	
   102	    def __repr__(self) -> str:
   103	        return f"{self.__class__.__name__}(section={self._section.key}, overrides={self.overrides!r})"
   104

"""
Script to reproduce the multiple env substitution issue in tox 4.3
"""

import tempfile
import os
from pathlib import Path
from tox.config.cli.parser import Parsed
from tox.config.main import Config
from tox.config.source.tox_ini import ToxIni


def test_multiple_env_substitution():
    """Test case that reproduces the multiple env substitution issue"""
    
    # Create a temporary tox.ini file with the problematic configuration
    tox_content = """[tox]
requires =
    tox == 4.3.1

[testenv]
passenv =
    USER
    HOME
allowlist_externals =
    echo
commands =
    echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}" "{env:USER}"
"""
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp_dir:
        tmp_path = Path(tmp_dir)
        tox_ini_file = tmp_path / "tox.ini"
        tox_ini_file.write_text(tox_content)
        
        print(f"Created tox.ini at: {tox_ini_file}")
        print("Content:")
        print(tox_content)
        
        # Set up environment variables
        os.environ["USER"] = "testuser"
        os.environ["HOME"] = "/home/testuser"
        
        try:
            # Try to load the configuration
            tox_ini = ToxIni(tox_ini_file)
            
            config = Config(
                tox_ini,
                options=Parsed(override=[]),
                root=tmp_path,
                pos_args=[],
                work_dir=tmp_path,
            )
            
            # Try to access the commands - this should trigger the replacement
            py_env = config.get_env("py")
            commands = py_env.conf["commands"]
            print("SUCCESS: Commands loaded successfully!")
            print(f"Commands: {commands}")
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            return False
            
    return True


def test_multiple_env_spread_across_commands():
    """Test case with multiple env vars spread across multiple commands"""
    
    # Create a temporary tox.ini file with the problematic configuration
    tox_content = """[tox]

[testenv]
passenv =
    USER
    HOME
allowlist_externals =
    echo
commands =
    echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}"

        tox_ini_file = tmp_path / "tox.ini"
        tox_ini_file.write_text(tox_content)

"""
Simple script to reproduce the issue by creating a tox.ini and running tox
"""

import tempfile
import os
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def test_multiple_env_substitution():
    """Create a simple tox.ini and try to run tox to reproduce the issue"""
    
    tox_content = """[testenv]
passenv =
    USER
    HOME
allowlist_externals =
    echo
commands =
    echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}" "{env:USER}"
"""
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp_dir:
        tmp_path = Path(tmp_dir)
        tox_ini_file = tmp_path / "tox.ini"
        tox_ini_file.write_text(tox_content)
        
        print(f"Created tox.ini at: {tox_ini_file}")
        print("Content:")
        print(tox_content)
        
        # Change to the temp directory and run tox
        old_cwd = os.getcwd()
        try:
            os.chdir(tmp_path)
            
            # Set up environment variables
            env = os.environ.copy()
            env["USER"] = "testuser"
            env["HOME"] = "/home/testuser"
            
            # Run tox with verbose output
            cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "tox", "-rvv"]
            print(f"Running: {' '.join(cmd)}")
            
            result = subprocess.run(
                cmd,
                capture_output=True,
                text=True,
                env=env,
                cwd=tmp_path
            )
            
            print("STDOUT:")
            print(result.stdout)
            print("STDERR:")  
            print(result.stderr)
            print(f"Return code: {result.returncode}")
            
            if "circular chain between set env" in result.stderr:
                print("❌ ISSUE REPRODUCED: Circular chain error detected!")
                return False
            elif result.returncode == 0:
                print("✅ SUCCESS: No error detected!")
                return True
            else:
                print("❓ Different error occurred")
                return False
                
        finally:
            os.chdir(old_cwd)


def test_multiple_commands():
    """Test with multiple commands"""
    
    tox_content = """[testenv]
passenv =
    USER
    HOME
allowlist_externals =
    echo
commands =
    echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}"
    echo "{env:USER}"
"""
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp_dir:
        tmp_path = Path(tmp_dir)
        tox_ini_file = tmp_path / "tox.ini"
        tox_ini_file.write_text(tox_content)

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze the problem:

5.1 What the problem is:
The issue is in the `replace_env` function in `/workspace/tox-dev__tox__4.3/src/tox/config/loader/ini/replace.py` at lines 317-325. When processing multiple `{env:VAR}` substitutions in the same string/command, the function incorrectly detects a circular dependency when the same variable is referenced multiple times.

5.2 Where the problem is located:
Lines 317-325 in `replace_env` function:
```python
if new_key not in conf_args.chain:  # check if set env
    conf_args.chain.append(new_key)
    env_conf = conf.get_env(conf_args.env_name)
    set_env: SetEnv = env_conf["set_env"]
    if key in set_env:
        return set_env.load(key, conf_args)
elif conf_args.chain[-1] != new_key:  # if there's a chain but only self-refers than use os.environ
    circular = ", ".join(i[4:] for i in conf_args.chain[conf_args.chain.index(new_key) :])
    raise MatchRecursionError(f"circular chain between set env {circular}")
```

5.3 How the test reproduces the issue:
When processing `"echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}" "{env:USER}"`, the replacement function:
1. Processes `{env:USER}` - adds `env:USER` to the chain
2. Processes `{env:HOME}` - adds `env:HOME` to the chain  
3. Processes `{env:USER}` again - sees `env:USER` is already in the chain, triggers the circular detection logic

5.4 Best practices to consider:
- The fix should distinguish between actual circular dependencies (A->B->A) and legitimate multiple references (A, B, A)
- Should not break existing circular dependency detection
- Should be minimal and focused

5.5 How to fix the problem:
The issue is in the logic that determines circular dependencies. The current code assumes that if a key is already in the chain, it's a circular dependency. However, legitimate cases can have the same key appearing multiple times in a chain without being circular.

The fix should check if the key is already in the chain BUT is not causing an actual cycle. A real cycle would be when we're trying to resolve key X and X is already in the current resolution path leading back to X.

Looking at the logic:
- Line 317: `if new_key not in conf_args.chain:` - if key not seen before, process normally
- Line 323: `elif conf_args.chain[-1] != new_key:` - if key IS in chain but not the immediate previous item, it's considered circular

The problem is that this logic treats any repeated key as circular, even when they're legitimately separate references.

A better approach would be to check if we're actually creating a cycle by seeing if the key appears in the current resolution path, not just anywhere in the chain.

Actually, looking more carefully, I think the intent of line 323 is to allow self-references (where a variable refers to itself) but catch other cycles. But the issue is that when we have USER -> HOME -> USER, it's treated as circular even though these are environment variables, not set_env variables.

Wait, let me re-read the code more carefully...

The issue seems to be that the code assumes that if a key is in the chain and it's not the last item, it's a circular dependency. But this is wrong for environment variables that come from `os.environ` rather than `set_env`.

Looking at the flow:
1. Process `{env:USER}` - not in chain, add to chain, check set_env (not there), check os.environ (found) -> return value
2. Process `{env:HOME}` - not in chain, add to chain, check set_env (not there), check os.environ (found) -> return value  
3. Process `{env:USER}` - IS in chain, check if last item (no, last item is HOME) -> raise circular error

But the issue is that USER and HOME are environment variables, not set_env variables, so they shouldn't be tracked in the chain for circular dependency detection at all!

Looking at the code again:
- Lines 317-322: Handle the case where the key is not in the chain or is a set_env variable
- Lines 323-325: Handle the case where key IS in chain and detect circular dependencies for set_env

But the problem is that environment variables from os.environ are being tracked in the chain when they shouldn't be, because they can't create circular dependencies (they're just static values).

The fix should be: only track set_env variables in the chain for circular dependency detection, not os.environ variables.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me examine the code more carefully to understand the exact flow:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/tox-dev__tox__4.3/src/tox/config/loader/ini/replace.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[310, 335]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/tox-dev__tox__4.3/src/tox/config/loader/ini/replace.py:

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I see the issue clearly! The problem is in the logic flow:

1. When processing `{env:USER}`, it adds `env:USER` to `conf_args.chain` (line 318) regardless of whether it's a set_env variable or an os.environ variable
2. When processing `{env:HOME}`, it adds `env:HOME` to `conf_args.chain` (line 318) 
3. When processing `{env:USER}` again, it finds `env:USER` already in the chain and raises the circular error

The issue is that the code always adds the key to the chain (line 318) but only checks for set_env variables (lines 321-322). If the key is not in set_env, it falls through to check os.environ, but the key is still in the chain for future lookups.

The fix should be: **Only add keys to the chain if they are actually set_env variables**, not for os.environ variables.

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>This test shows that circular dependency detection is meant for `set_env` variables, not for `os.environ` variables. The test expects success (line 110) but also expects the circular error to be caught and reported (lines 111-112).

So the chain tracking is specifically for detecting circular dependencies in `set_env` variables. Environment variables from `os.environ` should not be tracked in the chain because they can't create circular dependencies.

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION: Implementing the Solution

The fix is to only add keys to the chain if they are actually `set_env` variables. For `os.environ` variables, we should not track them in the chain.<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/tox-dev__tox__4.3/src/tox/config/loader/ini/replace.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def replace_env(conf: Config, args: list[str], conf_args: ConfigLoadArgs) -> str:
    if not args or not args[0]:
        raise MatchError("No variable name was supplied in {env} substitution")
    key = args[0]
    new_key = f"env:{key}"

    if conf_args.env_name is not None:  # on core no set env support # pragma: no branch
        if new_key not in conf_args.chain:  # check if set env
            conf_args.chain.append(new_key)
            env_conf = conf.get_env(conf_args.env_name)
            set_env: SetEnv = env_conf["set_env"]
            if key in set_env:
                return set_env.load(key, conf_args)
        elif conf_args.chain[-1] != new_key:  # if there's a chain but only self-refers than use os.environ
            circular = ", ".join(i[4:] for i in conf_args.chain[conf_args.chain.index(new_key) :])
            raise MatchRecursionError(f"circular chain between set env {circular}")

    if key in os.environ:
        return os.environ[key]

    return "" if len(args) == 1 else ARG_DELIMITER.join(args[1:])</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def replace_env(conf: Config, args: list[str], conf_args: ConfigLoadArgs) -> str:

"""
Test that circular dependency detection still works for actual set_env circular dependencies
"""

import tempfile
import os
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def test_actual_circular_dependency():
    """Test that actual circular dependencies in set_env are still detected"""
    
    tox_content = """[testenv]
package = skip
set_env =
    a = {env:b}
    b = {env:a}
commands =
    echo "This should not run"
"""
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp_dir:
        tmp_path = Path(tmp_dir)
        tox_ini_file = tmp_path / "tox.ini"
        tox_ini_file.write_text(tox_content)
        
        print(f"Testing actual circular dependency...")
        print(f"Created tox.ini at: {tox_ini_file}")
        print("Content:")
        print(tox_content)
        
        # Change to the temp directory and run tox
        old_cwd = os.getcwd()
        try:
            os.chdir(tmp_path)
            
            # Run tox 
            cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "tox", "-rvv"]
            print(f"Running: {' '.join(cmd)}")
            
            result = subprocess.run(
                cmd,
                capture_output=True,
                text=True,
                cwd=tmp_path
            )
            
            print("STDOUT:")
            print(result.stdout)
            print("STDERR:")  
            print(result.stderr)
            print(f"Return code: {result.returncode}")
            
            if "circular chain between set env" in result.stderr:
                print("✅ GOOD: Circular dependency correctly detected!")
                return True
            elif result.returncode == 0:
                print("❌ BAD: Circular dependency was not detected!")
                return False
            else:
                print("❓ Different error occurred")
                return False
                
        finally:
            os.chdir(old_cwd)

When we try to resolve `{env:MAGIC}`, it should:
1. Look up MAGIC in os.environ → gets `{env:TRAGIC}`
2. Try to resolve `{env:TRAGIC}` → looks up TRAGIC in os.environ → gets `{env:MAGIC}`

- For set_env variables: we want to track chains to detect circular dependencies between set_env definitions
- For os.environ variables: we want to track recursion during expansion to prevent infinite loops, but we don't want to track cross-variable references permanently

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>I see the issue now. My fix is correct for the main problem (not tracking os.environ variables in set_env circular detection), but I broke the recursion detection for environment variable expansion itself.

The problem is more subtle. Let me re-read the original logic:

1. Check if `new_key` is not in `conf_args.chain`
2. If not in chain, add it to chain and check if it's a set_env variable
3. If it IS in chain but not the last item, it's a circular dependency in set_env

But there's also implicit recursion detection happening in the `replace` function itself via the `depth` parameter that limits to `MAX_REPLACE_DEPTH`.

The failing test suggests that when expanding environment variables that reference other environment variables, there should be recursion detection that stops the expansion and returns the literal value.

Looking at the test expectation: when we have `MAGIC="{env:TRAGIC}"` and `TRAGIC="{env:MAGIC}"`, resolving `{env:MAGIC}` should return `"{env:MAGIC}"` (the literal string), not get stuck in an infinite loop.

The recursion detection happens in the `replace` function at line 56:
```python
if depth > MAX_REPLACE_DEPTH:
    raise MatchRecursionError(f"Could not expand {value} after recursing {depth} frames")
```

And this is caught at lines 204-206:
```python
except MatchRecursionError as err:
    LOGGER.warning(str(err))
    return replace_value
```

So the flow should be:
1. `{env:MAGIC}` -> look up MAGIC in os.environ -> get `"{env:TRAGIC}"`
2. Expand `"{env:TRAGIC}"` -> look up TRAGIC in os.environ -> get `"{env:MAGIC}"`
3. Expand `"{env:MAGIC}"` -> look up MAGIC in os.environ -> get `"{env:TRAGIC}"`  
4. This continues until MAX_REPLACE_DEPTH is exceeded
5. The recursion error is caught and the last attempted expansion (`"{env:MAGIC}"`) is returned

But my change moved where the chain tracking happens, which may have affected this.

[Command finished with exit code 1]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>I can see from the captured log that the recursion detection is working (`Could not expand {env:TRAGIC} after recursing 101 frames`), but the final result is wrong. 

The issue is that when the recursion limit is hit, it should return the original string that was being expanded, not the intermediate result.

Looking at lines 204-206:
```python
except MatchRecursionError as err:
    LOGGER.warning(str(err))
    return replace_value
```

The `replace_value` here is the last attempted expansion, which is `"{env:TRAGIC}"` instead of the original `"{env:MAGIC}"`.

But this is not the main issue I need to fix. The main issue is that I broke the chain tracking logic. Let me re-think this.

The real problem is conceptual. I moved the `chain.append(new_key)` inside the `if key in set_env:` condition, but the original logic was:
1. Always append to chain when first encountering a key (whether set_env or os.environ)
2. Only do set_env loading if it's actually a set_env variable
3. Detect circular dependencies in the chain

But for os.environ variables, they shouldn't contribute to circular dependency detection because they can't create circular dependencies (they're just static values).

However, there's still a need to track recursion during expansion of os.environ variables themselves.

Let me look at this differently. The chain is meant to track set_env circular dependencies. My fix was conceptually correct - only track set_env variables in the chain. But I broke the recursion detection for environment variables.

Actually, let me check if the recursion detection for environment variables was working in the original code. Maybe the test was passing by accident?

Let me look at what the original code was doing step by step for the failing test:

1. `{env:MAGIC}` - MAGIC not in chain, add to chain, check set_env (no), check os.environ (yes, value=`{env:TRAGIC}`)
2. Expand `{env:TRAGIC}` - TRAGIC not in chain, add to chain, check set_env (no), check os.environ (yes, value=`{env:MAGIC}`)
3. Expand `{env:MAGIC}` - MAGIC IS in chain, but it's the last item? No, last item is TRAGIC.
4. So it raises circular error.

Wait, that doesn't seem right. The chain progression should be:
1. Chain: []
2. Process MAGIC: not in chain → add to chain → Chain: [`env:MAGIC`]  
3. Process TRAGIC: not in chain → add to chain → Chain: [`env:MAGIC`, `env:TRAGIC`]
4. Process MAGIC again: in chain, last item is TRAGIC, not MAGIC → circular error

But that's wrong logic! The fact that MAGIC appeared before shouldn't make it circular when processing os.environ variables.

The issue is that the original code conflates two different concepts:
1. Circular dependencies between set_env variables (which should be detected)
2. Recursion during environment variable expansion (which should be limited by depth, not chain tracking)

My fix was on the right track, but I need to be more careful about when to add to the chain.

Let me revise the approach: Only add to the chain when we're actually dealing with set_env variables that could participate in circular dependencies.<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/tox-dev__tox__4.3/src/tox/config/loader/ini/replace.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def replace_env(conf: Config, args: list[str], conf_args: ConfigLoadArgs) -> str:
    if not args or not args[0]:
        raise MatchError("No variable name was supplied in {env} substitution")
    key = args[0]
    new_key = f"env:{key}"

    if conf_args.env_name is not None:  # on core no set env support # pragma: no branch
        if new_key not in conf_args.chain:  # check if set env
            env_conf = conf.get_env(conf_args.env_name)
            set_env: SetEnv = env_conf["set_env"]
            if key in set_env:
                conf_args.chain.append(new_key)
                return set_env.load(key, conf_args)
        elif conf_args.chain[-1] != new_key:  # if there's a chain but only self-refers than use os.environ
            circular = ", ".join(i[4:] for i in conf_args.chain[conf_args.chain.index(new_key) :])
            raise MatchRecursionError(f"circular chain between set env {circular}")

    if key in os.environ:
        return os.environ[key]

```python
    LOGGER.warning(str(err))
```

import tempfile
from pathlib import Path
from tests.config.loader.ini.replace.conftest import replace_one
from tox.pytest import MonkeyPatch

def test_debug():
    mp = MonkeyPatch()
    mp.setenv('TRAGIC', '{env:MAGIC}')
    mp.setenv('MAGIC', '{env:TRAGIC}')
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp_dir:
        tmp_path = Path(tmp_dir)
        tox_ini_file = tmp_path / 'tox.ini'
        tox_ini_file.write_text('[testenv:py]\nenv={env:MAGIC}\n')
        print('Chain before:', [])
        
        from tox.config.cli.parser import Parsed
        from tox.config.main import Config
        from tox.config.source.tox_ini import ToxIni
        
        tox_ini = ToxIni(tox_ini_file)
        config = Config(tox_ini, options=Parsed(override=[]), root=tmp_path, pos_args=[], work_dir=tmp_path)
        loader = config.get_env('py').loaders[0]
        args = type('ConfigLoadArgs', (), {'chain': [], 'name': 'a', 'env_name': 'a'})()
        
        from tox.config.loader.ini.replace import replace_env
        result = loader.load(key='env', of_type=str, conf=config, factory=None, args=args)
        print('Result:', repr(result))
        print('Chain after:', args.chain)

1. Keep circular dependency detection for environment variables that reference each other (like the test case)


Actually, let me trace through what should happen:

For the failing test:
1. Process `{env:MAGIC}` - MAGIC not in chain → add to chain[`env:MAGIC`], look up in os.environ → get `"{env:TRAGIC}"`
2. Need to expand `"{env:TRAGIC}"` → call replace recursively 
3. Process `{env:TRAGIC}` - TRAGIC not in chain → add to chain[`env:MAGIC`, `env:TRAGIC`], look up in os.environ → get `"{env:MAGIC}"`
4. Need to expand `"{env:MAGIC}"` → call replace recursively
5. Process `{env:MAGIC}` - MAGIC IS in chain at position 0, last item is TRAGIC ≠ MAGIC → circular error

This correctly detects the cycle.

For our bug case:
1. Process `{env:USER}` - USER not in chain → add to chain[`env:USER`], look up in os.environ → get value
2. Process `{env:HOME}` - HOME not in chain → add to chain[`env:USER`, `env:HOME`], look up in os.environ → get value  
3. Process `{env:USER}` - USER IS in chain at position 0, last item is HOME ≠ USER → circular error ❌

The issue is that step 3 incorrectly flags this as circular, but there's no actual cycle - we're just referencing USER again after referencing HOME.

The problem is that the circular detection logic assumes that if a variable appears in the chain and it's not the immediate predecessor, there's a cycle. But this is wrong when the variable is being referenced independently.

I think the issue is conceptual. The chain should represent active dependencies being resolved, not just a history of what's been seen.

In my code:

Let me try a different approach. Keep the chain tracking for all variables (like the original), but improve the circular detection logic to be smarter about what constitutes a real cycle.

Actually, let me step back and understand the semantics better. What exactly should constitute a circular dependency?

1. **Real circular dependency**: When expanding variable A, we need variable B, and when expanding B, we need A again. This creates an infinite loop.

2. **Legitimate repeated reference**: Referencing the same variable multiple times independently, like `echo {env:USER} {env:HOME} {env:USER}`. This should work fine.

The key difference is:
- Real cycle: A(expansion) → B(expansion) → A(expansion) 
- Legitimate repetition: A(reference), B(reference), A(reference)

But in our replacement logic, everything looks like expansion. So we need a way to distinguish.

Actually, let me re-read the original error more carefully. The error says "circular chain between set env USER, HOME". But USER and HOME are NOT set_env variables in our test case - they're passed through passenv.

    if not args or not args[0]:
        raise MatchError("No variable name was supplied in {env} substitution")
    key = args[0]
    new_key = f"env:{key}"

    if conf_args.env_name is not None:  # on core no set env support # pragma: no branch
        if new_key not in conf_args.chain:  # check if set env
            conf_args.chain.append(new_key)
            env_conf = conf.get_env(conf_args.env_name)
            set_env: SetEnv = env_conf["set_env"]
            if key in set_env:
                return set_env.load(key, conf_args)
        elif conf_args.chain[-1] != new_key:  # if there's a chain but only self-refers than use os.environ
            # Check if this is a real circular dependency or just a repeated reference
            # Find the last occurrence of new_key in the chain
            last_index = -1
            for i in range(len(conf_args.chain) - 2, -1, -1):  # Search backwards, excluding the last item
                if conf_args.chain[i] == new_key:
                    last_index = i
                    break
            
            # If we found a previous occurrence, check if there's a real cycle
            if last_index != -1:
                # Extract the sub-chain from last occurrence to end
                sub_chain = conf_args.chain[last_index:]
                # A real cycle would mean we can traverse the chain and get back to where we started
                # For now, we'll use the original logic but be more careful
                circular = ", ".join(i[4:] for i in conf_args.chain[conf_args.chain.index(new_key) :])
                raise MatchRecursionError(f"circular chain between set env {circular}")

        return os.environ[key]

   310	def replace_env(conf: Config, args: list[str], conf_args: ConfigLoadArgs) -> str:
   311	    if not args or not args[0]:
   312	        raise MatchError("No variable name was supplied in {env} substitution")
   313	    key = args[0]
   314	    new_key = f"env:{key}"
   315	
   316	    if conf_args.env_name is not None:  # on core no set env support # pragma: no branch
   317	        env_conf = conf.get_env(conf_args.env_name)
   318	        set_env: SetEnv = env_conf["set_env"]
   319	        if key in set_env:
   320	            # Only track set_env variables for circular dependency detection
   321	            if new_key not in conf_args.chain:  # check if set env
   322	                conf_args.chain.append(new_key)
   323	                return set_env.load(key, conf_args)
   324	            elif conf_args.chain[-1] != new_key:  # if there's a chain but only self-refers than use os.environ
   325	                circular = ", ".join(i[4:] for i in conf_args.chain[conf_args.chain.index(new_key) :])
   326	                raise MatchRecursionError(f"circular chain between set env {circular}")
   327	        # For non-set_env variables, fall through to os.environ check below
   328	
   329	    if key in os.environ:
   330	        return os.environ[key]
   331

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I understand the issue better. My approach of not tracking os.environ variables in the chain breaks the circular dependency detection for environment variables that actually do reference each other.

But I also can't go back to the original approach because it treats legitimate repeated references as circular.

Let me think of a compromise approach. What if I track ALL variables in the chain (like original) but make the circular detection smarter?

The key insight is: a circular dependency should only be detected when we're in the middle of expanding a variable and we encounter a reference to a variable that's already in our current expansion path.

But the current logic flags ANY repeated appearance as circular, which is wrong.

Let me look at the failing test case again and understand exactly what path it takes:

For `TRAGIC="{env:MAGIC}"` and `MAGIC="{env:TRAGIC}"` with input `"{env:MAGIC}"`:

1. Process `{env:MAGIC}` → chain=[], not in chain → chain=[`env:MAGIC`], os.environ lookup → get `"{env:TRAGIC}"`
2. Expand `"{env:TRAGIC}"` → chain=[`env:MAGIC`], TRAGIC not in chain → chain=[`env:MAGIC`, `env:TRAGIC`], os.environ lookup → get `"{env:MAGIC}"`  
3. Expand `"{env:MAGIC}"` → chain=[`env:MAGIC`, `env:TRAGIC`], MAGIC IS in chain at index 0, last item is TRAGIC ≠ MAGIC → circular error

This IS a real circular dependency, so it should be flagged.

For our bug case with `"{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}" "{env:USER}"`:

1. Process `{env:USER}` → chain=[], not in chain → chain=[`env:USER`], os.environ lookup → get value
2. Process `{env:HOME}` → chain=[`env:USER`], HOME not in chain → chain=[`env:USER`, `env:HOME`], os.environ lookup → get value
3. Process `{env:USER}` → chain=[`env:USER`, `env:HOME`], USER IS in chain at index 0, last item is HOME ≠ USER → circular error ❌

The third step incorrectly flags this as circular.

The difference is that in step 3, we're NOT in the middle of expanding USER - we're processing an independent reference to USER.

But how can the code distinguish between these cases? Both look the same from the perspective of the `replace_env` function.

Actually, wait. Maybe the issue is that I'm misunderstanding the workflow. Let me think about when `replace_env` gets called.

In our command `echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}" "{env:USER}"`, the entire command string gets processed as one unit, and `replace_env` gets called three times in sequence for each placeholder.

But in the circular case `TRAGIC="{env:MAGIC}"` where we resolve `"{env:MAGIC}"`, the expansion happens recursively - `replace_env` calls `replace` which calls `replace_env` again.

So the difference is:
- Independent references: `replace_env` called multiple times at the same recursion level
- Circular expansion: `replace_env` calls itself recursively through the `replace` function

But that's not quite right either, because even independent references go through the `replace` function.

Let me step back and think about this more fundamentally. 

The chain represents the current expansion stack. When we have a true circular dependency, the chain will show the dependency path that leads back to itself.

Maybe the issue is simpler. What if the circular detection logic is just wrong?

Looking at line 323: `elif conf_args.chain[-1] != new_key:`

This says: "if the key is already in the chain AND the last item in the chain is NOT the same key, then it's circular."

But this logic is flawed. Consider:
- Chain: [`env:A`, `env:B`]  
- Processing: `env:A` again
- `new_key` (`env:A`) is in chain? Yes, at index 0
- Last item in chain (`env:B`) != `new_key` (`env:A`)? Yes
- Therefore, circular? According to current logic, yes.

But this is wrong! There's no circular dependency here. We processed A, then B, now we're processing A again independently.

The correct logic should be: there's a circular dependency if we're trying to expand a variable that's already in our current expansion path, meaning there's a cycle in the directed graph of dependencies.

A better approach might be to check if we can reach the current variable by following the chain backwards.

But actually, let me look at what the original working behavior was. In the original code, the failing test was working. Let me trace through that case with the original logic:

Chain evolution in failing test with original code:
1. Process `{env:MAGIC}` → chain=[], not in chain → chain=[`env:MAGIC`], os.environ lookup → get `"{env:TRAGIC}"`
2. Expand `"{env:TRAGIC}"` → chain=[`env:MAGIC`], TRAGIC not in chain → chain=[`env:MAGIC`, `env:TRAGIC`], os.environ lookup → get `"{env:MAGIC}"`
3. Expand `"{env:MAGIC}"` → chain=[`env:MAGIC`, `env:TRAGIC`], MAGIC IS in chain, last item is TRAGIC ≠ MAGIC → circular error ✓ (correct)

Chain evolution in our bug case with original code:  
1. Process `{env:USER}` → chain=[], not in chain → chain=[`env:USER`], os.environ lookup → get value
2. Process `{env:HOME}` → chain=[`env:USER`], HOME not in chain → chain=[`env:USER`, `env:HOME`], os.environ lookup → get value
3. Process `{env:USER}` → chain=[`env:USER`, `env:HOME`], USER IS in chain, last item is HOME ≠ USER → circular error ❌ (incorrect)

So the original logic has the same flaw!

This means the test was passing by accident because of some other mechanism stopping the recursion, not because the circular detection was correct.

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Excellent! My fix is working for the main issue. Now I just need to fix the failing test. The issue with the failing test is that my approach of not tracking os.environ variables in the chain broke the circular dependency detection for environment variables that actually DO reference each other.

But I think the real solution is more nuanced. I need to track ALL variables in the chain for proper recursion detection, but I need to fix the FALSE POSITIVE circular detection.

Let me think about what the difference is between a TRUE circular dependency and a FALSE POSITIVE:

TRUE circular dependency (should be detected):
- `{env:MAGIC}` → expands to `"{env:TRAGIC}"` → expands to `"{env:MAGIC}"` → cycle!

FALSE positive (should NOT be detected):
- Process `{env:USER}` independently 
- Process `{env:HOME}` independently
- Process `{env:USER}` independently again - no cycle, just repeated reference

The key insight is that in the FALSE positive case, we're processing independent references at the same logical level, whereas in the TRUE circular case, we're in a recursive expansion.

But actually, both cases go through recursive calls to `replace()`. The difference might be more subtle.

Let me look at this from a different perspective. What if the issue is that the chain state is being shared across independent resolutions?

Actually, let me check if there's a simpler fix. What if I just make the circular detection more conservative?

Looking at line 323 in my current fix:
```python
elif conf_args.chain[-1] != new_key:  # if there's a chain but only self-refers than use os.environ
```

This logic says: if the key is in the chain AND it's not the last item, then it's circular. But this is too aggressive.

A better approach might be to only flag as circular if we can trace a dependency path from the current key back to itself through the chain.

But actually, let me first understand why the test was passing before. Maybe the issue is that the test wasn't actually testing what I thought it was testing.

Case 1 (Should NOT be flagged - our bug report):
- Command: `echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}" "{env:USER}"`  
- Processing sequence: USER (independent), HOME (independent), USER (independent)
- Chain evolution: [] → [`USER`] → [`USER`, `HOME`] → [`USER`, `HOME`, `USER`] 
- When processing third USER: USER is in chain (index 0) and last item is HOME ≠ USER → currently flagged as circular ❌

Wait, let me re-read the issue description more carefully. The problem is with:

```
commands =
    echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}" "{env:USER}"
```

This is a SINGLE command line with multiple placeholders. So it gets processed as one unit.

But:

```
commands =
    echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}"
    echo "{env:USER}"
```

These are TWO separate command lines, each processed independently.

So in both cases, each individual string gets processed independently by the replacement logic.

But within a single string like `"echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}" "{env:USER}""`, all the placeholders are replaced in sequence using the same `conf_args` object, which means the same chain.

That's the key! The chain persists across all replacements within a single string, which causes the FALSE POSITIVE.

In contrast, in the circular test case, the chain also persists across the recursive expansions, which correctly detects the cycle.

So the fundamental issue is that the chain is designed to track dependencies WITHIN a single replacement operation, but it's being used across multiple independent references within the same string.

But this is how it's supposed to work - the chain SHOULD track the expansion path for circular dependency detection.

The real issue might be that the circular detection logic is too simplistic.

Let me look at what the ideal behavior should be:

For `"echo "{env:USER}" "{env:HOME}" "{env:USER}"`:
1. Process `{env:USER}` → USER not in chain → add USER, lookup → get value, remove USER from chain???
2. Process `{env:HOME}` → HOME not in chain → add HOME, lookup → get value, remove HOME from chain???  
3. Process `{env:USER}` → USER not in chain → add USER, lookup → get value

But that would break the circular detection.

Alternatively:
1. Process `{env:USER}` → USER not in chain → add USER, lookup → get value (keep USER in chain)
2. Process `{env:HOME}` → HOME not in chain → add HOME, lookup → get value (keep both in chain)
3. Process `{env:USER}` → USER IS in chain, but there's no dependency relationship → ???

The issue is distinguishing between:
- Dependency relationship: when processing X, we NEED to expand Y 
- Independent reference: we just want the value of Y

But in both cases, we call `replace_env` the same way.

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Good, my fix is restored. Now let me think about how to fix the failing test. The issue is that my approach prevents circular dependency detection for environment variables entirely.

But actually, let me step back and think about whether the failing test is actually testing the right thing. 

Looking at the test again:
```python
def test_replace_env_var_circular_flip_flop(replace_one: ReplaceOne, monkeypatch: MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    """Replacement values will not infinitely loop back and forth"""
    monkeypatch.setenv("TRAGIC", "{env:MAGIC}")
    monkeypatch.setenv("MAGIC", "{env:TRAGIC}")
    result = replace_one("{env:MAGIC}")
    assert result == "{env:MAGIC}"
```

This test is saying that when we have circular environment variable definitions, the system should detect this and return the original placeholder instead of getting stuck in an infinite loop.

But with my fix, since I'm not tracking os.environ variables in the chain, there's no early circular detection. The system relies on the recursion depth limit instead.

The issue is that when the recursion limit is hit, it returns the LAST attempted expansion instead of the ORIGINAL placeholder.

So I think the real fix is to make the recursion handling return the original value. But that would require changing the recursion handling logic, which is more invasive.

Alternatively, I could make a targeted fix just for this specific case. But let me first see if there's a simpler approach.

Actually, let me look at the failing test more carefully. The test comment says "Replacement values will not infinitely loop back and forth", and it expects the ORIGINAL placeholder to be returned.
